TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 14, 2014 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON 
The first order of business is to ele ct an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  Past 
practice has been that the previous year’s president conducts this part 
of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of  business is for the acting chairperson  to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temporary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT 
The acting chairperson declares nominations in order for the office of 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not be 
seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the nomination.)  
Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT 
The newly elected p resident declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY 
The president declares nominations in order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER 
According to Board Policy, the treasurer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served a s treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue.

     6. C.    01.14.14 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to  time, come into 
the hands of Mark S. Rajter , Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public 
monies belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the 
State or otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to 
be designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE  BE IT RESOLVED  that said Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , is 
hereby directed to deposit all public monies, including tax monies now in or 
coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the following 
depository banks: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Citizen’s Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. 
Rajter, and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. 
Rajter, or the Superintendent , Barbara A. Fowler, who may sign on the General 
Fund account and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The 
Troy Career Center Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District f or the 
account established at Bank of America for purposes of paying remaining health 
care claims may be withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of  the 
Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , and for checks over $10,000, the additional 
handwritten signature of Jasen Witt , Assistant Superintendent for Human 
Resources or Monica Papasian, Finance Director; and 
11
     6. C.    01.14.14 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
account established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and 
dependent care flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next 
Generation Enrollment, Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the 
Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter or Finance Director, Monica Papasian, and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , may open or 
close accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of 
the District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested 
according to Board Policy D-1103; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine 
money from more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment  
authorized by subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School 
Code; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the funds of the District may be invested with 
the following institutions: 
Bank of America 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Charter One Bank 
Citizens Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code.

      2. A.   01.14.14 
2.  RECOGNITION       
A.    Board of Education Recognition 
Each January, the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of 
Education.  
Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board members who are 
tireless, professional, and passionate about improving the  
opportunities for our students and our families . Tonight, we would 
like to recognize our Board of Education  members with a gift  that 
represents the community they give so much to. 
The gift before you is a token of appreciation that will allow Board 
members to enjoy what is unique about Troy .  From  a meal out at 
one of our newest restaurants to a few keepsakes showing our city 
pride….we hope you enjoy this opportunity for our city to give back 
to you - a group that has given so much to our students and staff. 
Thank you for all you do.   
Present gift.

BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
BOARD MEETING     MINUTES   DECEMBER 3, 2013 
       TROY HIGH SCHOOL  
  President Philippart called the meeting to order at 7:00 
p.m. 
In  addition  to  Ms.  Philippart,  present  were  Board 
members  Edmunds,  Fleming,  Hauff,  Miletti,  and 
Schmidt.   Ms.  Underwood  was  absent.    Also  present 
were  Superintendent  Fowler;  administrators  Keeling,  
Lesser,  Machesky,  Mull,  Rajter,  and  Witt  and 
approximately 100 additional members of the audience.  
Ms.  Philippart  began  the  meeting  stating  that  student 
representatives  who  were  in  attendance  at  this 
evening’s meeting would be moved to the first item on 
the  agenda  so  that  the  entire  audience  would  be 
present to hear their reports.   
Michaela  Smith,  student  representative  from  Athens 
Student Representatives  
High School, reported that the Athens fall play opens on 
Friday  and  that  their  holiday  concerts  for  band, 
orchestra  and  choir  will  be  held  in  the  weeks  to  come.  
She indicated that winter sports have begun this week.  
She  added  that  their  canned  food  drive  starts  Monday 
and  that  Project  Lead  continues  to  collect  winter  coats 
for people in need.  She stated that Athens students will 
have a busy academic schedule until the break.   
Patty  Durell,  student  representative  from  Troy  high 
School,  reported  that  their  holiday  band  concert  and 
orchestra  concert  featuring  the  choir  will  be  held  next 
Thursday.   The sophomore class has been working hard 
planning the Snow Glow Dance, which is this Friday.  She 
also  indicated  that  numerous  clubs  are  beginning  to 
raise  money  for  a  variety  of  charities  and  English 
teacher,  Mrs.  Clark  and  her  Personal  Communication 
classes  have  adopted  a wing  in  a  local  hospital and  are 
collecting toys for children with cancer for the holidays.  
She  added  that  the  student  government  will  be  selling 
typhoon relief bracelets to help support the Philippines 
next week.   She stated that all students are getting into 
5
the holiday spirit and are excited for the break in three 
weeks!    
Student  representatives  from  IA‐East  and  Niles 
Community High School were not in attendance. 
ADDITIONS AND DELETIONS  None.

regarding the lack of a teacher contract.

Board regarding teacher negotiations.   
indicating  he  supports  the  teachers  but  also  supports 
the fiscal responsibility of the District.

addressed the Board regarding the teachers’ contract.

Board regarding teacher negotiations. 
regarding teacher negotiations.

regarding the lack of a contract for Troy teachers.

teachers  in  his  children’s  lives  and  the  need  to  settle 
their contract.

CURRICULUM 
Mrs.  Anne  Mull,  Director,  Assessment  and  Instruction, 
accountability  reports  including  the  new  Michigan 
School  Accountability  Scorecards  for  districts  and 
Reward, Focus and Priority Schools.   The Scorecard was 
released as the result of Michigan receiving flexibility to 
the  federal  No  Child  Left  Behind  (NCLB)  Act  from  the 
U.S.  Department  of  Education.  As  a  result,  Adequate 
Yearly  Progress  (AYP)  no  longer  will  be  reported.   The 
new  color‐coded  system  is  to  be  used  as  a  diagnostic 
tool  to  help  identify  strengths  and  weaknesses  and 
provide  greater  transparency  and  detail  on  multiple 
levels of school performance. 
Mr. Schmidt inquired about the bottom 30% and the gap 
between the top and bottom.   Mrs. Mull explained that 
Ms. Philippart talked about the possibility of legislature 
giving  districts  their  own  scorecards  relative  to  their 
community  in  the  future.   They  talked  the  value  of  the 
color‐coded system.   They also discussed evaluating the 
district’s  programs  to  make  sure  all  students  are 
successful.   Additional  discussion  was  held  relating  to 
college  and  career  readiness  of  students  in  the  district 
and how we track that.   
BUSINESS 
2014  School  Building  and  Site 
Education  of  the  Troy  School  District  does  hereby 
authorize  the  issuance  of  bonds  designated  the  “2014 
School  Building  and  Site  Bonds”  in  an  amount  not  to

 7. A. 01.14.14 
7.     CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105  
          Elementary 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts are required to 
pass a resolution each school year indicating whether or not they plan to 
implement a Schools of Choice program for nonresident students who 
attend another district within their county.  I t would be appropriate at 
this time for the Board of Education to consider adopting a resolution for 
Schools of Choice  Section 105 for 50 kindergarten/25 first -grade (total 
students including non Oakland County residents ) who attend a school 
district within Oakland County. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School  
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105 of the State School Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
limited Schools of Choice program  for the 201 4-2015 school year ; for 50 
nonresident kindergarten and 25 nonresident first-grade students who attend a 
school district within Oakland County. 
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

7. CURRICULUM 
C. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105 
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  shall pass a resolution 
each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  It 
is anticipated that seat s will be available for the 20 14-2015 school year for 
grades 6 - 8 at Baker Middle School .  It would be appropriate at this time to 
consider adopting a Schools of Choice section 105  resolution for 26 (total 
students including non Oakland  county residents) non-resident 6th – 8th grade 
students who attend a district within Oakland County. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION

7. CURRICULUM 
D. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105c 
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  shall pass a resolutio n 
each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  It 
is anticipated that seat s will be available for the 201 4-2015 school year for 
grades 6 - 8 at Baker Middle School .  It would be appropriate at this time to 
consider adopti ng a Schools of Choice section 105c resolution for 26 (total 
students including Oakland County residents ) non-resident 6th – 8th grade 
students who attend a district in another county, if that intermediate school 
district is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School District boundaries.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________

District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105c  of the State School Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
limited Schools of Choice program  for th e 2014-2015 school year for 26 non-
resident students who attend a school district in another county if that 
Intermediate School District is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School 
District boundaries for the purposes of attending grades 6 – 8 at Baker Middle 
School.

A. CONSIDERATION:  NSBA Conference Attendance 
As required by Board Policy B -2001, Workshops and Conferences, 
“…expenditure of district funds for members for out -of-state meetings 
must have prior approval by resolution.”  Board President, Ms. Philippart, 
has indicated her desire to attend the National School Boards Association’s 
Annual Federal Relations Network (FRN) Conference, which will be held in 
Washington, D.C., from February 2 – 4, 2014. Troy Schools was one of six 
districts selected by Oakland County School Board Association to receive a 
$500 scholarship as reimbursement if  a Board member attends this 
conference.  We, therefore, ask you to consider the following resolution.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution.

   Supported by  
Be it therefore resolved  that the Board of Education takes action to approve 
Ms. Philippart’s attendance as representative of the Troy Board of Educa tion at the

in Washington, D.C., from February 2 - 4, 2014.

10.  OTHER 
B.   CONSIDERATION:  MASB Superintendent Search Contract Approval  
At the Tuesday, January 7, 2014,  Special Board Workshop,  the Board met with 
representatives from Michigan Association o f School Boards (MASB) to discuss the search 
for a new superintendent. MASB offers a service to assist local boards of education  in 
conducting custom-designed searches for a new superintendent.  Each search is 
individualized to meet the needs of the Board at a cost below other market rates.  The 
Troy Board would like to enter into a service contract with MASB to support the district as 
follows: 
 Supply Troy School District with trained, experienced and professional consultant to 
assist and guide Board throu gh all stages of the search and selection process 
professionally and efficiently; 
 Develop individualized process of search to best match needs of the Board by 
handling all details and advising Board of its options; 
 Enlist staff, parents and community members for selection criteria as desired; 
 Coordinate the entire process  - collecting information on candidates, keeping 
records and maintaining contact with educational agencies nationwide, university 
placement centers, educational journals and webpages; 
 Advise Board in every facet of selection process, including reaching consensus on 
decisions that must be made during search. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be it therefore reso lved that the Board of Education takes action to approve entering 
into a service contract with  Michigan Association of School Boards (MASB)  to conduct the 
search for a superintendent.
